L0: ## **CENTER FOR DRUG EVALUATION AND
## RESEARCH**
L1: *APPLICATION NUMBER:*
L2: **761033Orig1s000**
L3: **PHARMACOLOGY REVIEW(S)**
L4: ## Tertiary Pharmacology/Toxicology Review
L5: **Date:** March 18, 2016
**From:** Timothy J. McGovern, PhD, ODE Associate Director for Pharmacology and Toxicology, OND IO
**BLA:** 761033
**Agency receipt date:** March 30, 2015
**Drug:** Cinqair (Reslizumab) Injection
**Sponsor:** Teva Branded Pharmaceutical Products R&amp;D, Inc.
L6: **Indication:** Reduce exacerbations, relieve symptoms, and improve lung function in adults and adolescents (12 years of age and above) with asthma and elevated blood eosinophils who are inadequately controlled on inhaled corticosteroids.
L7: **Reviewing Division:** Division of Pulmonary, Allergy, and Rheumatology Products
L8: The primary pharmacology/toxicology reviewer and team leader concluded that the nonclinical data for Cinqair (Reslizumab) Injection support approval for the indication listed above.
L9: Cinqair is an intravenous (IV) injection product containing the active pharmaceutical ingredient reslizumab containing 100 mg reslizumab/10 mL vial. Reslizumab is a humanized monoclonal antibody (IgG4 kappa), specific for interleukin-5 (IL-5). The recommended dose of reslizumab is 3 mg/kg administered once every 4 weeks. The Established Pharmacologic Class (EPC) for reslizumab is “interleukin-5 antagonist (IgG4 kappa)”. Currently, the only other approved therapy for asthma that targets IL-5 is NUCALA (mepolizumab), an interleukin-5 antagonist monoclonal antibody (IgG1).
L10: Pivotal nonclinical studies were conducted in CD-1 mice and Cynomolgus monkeys; both were identified as relevant species in a series of pharmacology studies. In toxicology studies up to 6 months duration, no target organs of toxicity were identified in either mice or monkeys at an IV dose up to 25 mg/kg once every 4 weeks. Eosinophil counts were reduced in all treatment groups of mice. The NOAELs of 25 mg/kg provided a safety margin of approximately 3-fold compared to the recommended clinical dose based on systemic exposure comparisons.
L11: Studies in juvenile animals were conducted in CD-1 mice as part of a pre- and post-natal development study and in a stand-alone study in monkeys (1.4-2.3 years of age). In mice, a NOAEL of 10 mg/kg was identified based on clinical signs and death in one animal at the high dose of 25 mg/kg. In monkeys, the NOAEL was the high dose of 15 mg/kg.
L12: Genetic toxicity studies were not applicable for this therapeutic biologic protein though reslizumab was negative in two in vitro assays. A transgenic mouse (Tg.rasH2) carcinogenicity study was conducted with dose concurrence by the Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee (ECAC). No drug-related neoplasms were found after dosing up to 500 mg/kg IV once every two weeks.
L13: A complete battery of development and reproductive studies was conducted in CD-1 mice and New Zealand white rabbits; both were identified as relevant species. No effects on fertility or development parameters were identified at doses up to 50 mg/kg IV; exposure margins of approximately 6-fold were available in the mouse studies. A safety margin of 17-fold based on mg/kg comparisons was present in the rabbit study. Reslizumab was observed to cross the placenta of pregnant mice and was found in milk of lactating mice following dosing during pregnancy.
L14: **Conclusion:** I agree with the Division pharmacology/toxicology conclusion that this BLA can be approved from the pharmacology/toxicology perspective. The EPC for reslizumab is appropriate. I have discussed and am in agreement with labeling revisions proposed by the Division; the labeling follows the Pregnancy and Lactation Labeling Rule (PLLR) format and is consistent with current labeling recommendations.
L15: This is a representation of an electronic record that was signed electronically and this page is the manifestation of the electronic signature.
L16: /s/
L17: TIMOTHY J MCGOVERN
L18: 03/18/2016

Product: CINQAIR (reslizumab) Injection
Indication: For add-on maintenance treatment of patients with severe asthma 18 years and older, and with an eosinophilic phenotype.

L22: ## TABLE OF CONTENTS
L23: 1	EXECUTIVE SUMMARY	3

L24: ## 1 Executive Summary
L25: ## 1.1 Introduction
L26: This review evaluates the nonclinical sections of the proposed labeling for CINQAIR (reslizumab) Injection submitted by Teva Branded Pharmaceutical Products R&amp;D, Inc. on January 4, 2016, under BLA 761033. The following sections of the prescribing information were reviewed and are discussed herein: sections 8.1 "Pregnancy", 8.2 "Lactation", (b) (4) 10 "Overdosage", 11 "Description" (first paragraph only), 12.1 "Mechanism of Action", and 13 "Nonclinical Toxicology". An integrated review of the nonclinical program to support approval of this BLA was completed on December 16, 2015.
L27: ## 1.3 Recommendations
L28: ## 1.3.3 Labeling
L29: The following is the recommended language for the aforementioned sections of the prescribing information.
L30: ## 8.1 Pregnancy
L31: ## Risk Summary
L32: (b) (4) Monoclonal antibodies, such as reslizumab, are transported across the placenta in a linear fashion as pregnancy progresses; therefore, potential effects on a fetus are likely to be greater during the second and third trimester of pregnancy. (b) (4)
L33: (b) (4) In animal reproduction studies, there was no evidence of embryo-fetal adverse developmental effects with IV administration of reslizumab during organogenesis to pregnant mice and rabbits at doses that produced exposures up to approximately 6 times the exposure at the maximum recommended human dose (MRHD) in mice and approximately 17 times the exposure at the MRHD in rabbits [see Data].
L34: The estimated background risk of major birth defects and miscarriage for the indicated population(s) are unknown. In the U.S. general population, the estimated background risk of major birth defects and miscarriage in clinically recognized pregnancies is 2 to 4% and 15 to 20%, respectively.
L35: ## Clinical Considerations
L36: ## *Disease-Associated Maternal and/or Embryo-Fetal Risk*
L37: In women with poorly or moderately controlled asthma, evidence demonstrates that there is an increased risk of preeclampsia in the mother and prematurity, low birth weight, and small for gestational age in the neonate. The level of asthma control should be closely monitored in pregnant women and treatment adjusted as necessary to maintain optimal control.
L38: ## Data
L39: ## *Animal Data*
L40: In two separate embryo-fetal development studies, pregnant mice and rabbits received a single reslizumab dose during the period of organogenesis at 2, 10, and 50 mg/kg (0.4, 1.5, and 6 times the exposures achieved at the MRHD in mice on an AUC basis and 0.67, 3.3, and 17 times the exposures achieved at the MRHD in rabbits on a mg/kg basis). Reslizumab was not teratogenic in mice or rabbits. Embryo-fetal development of IL-5 deficient mice has been reported to be generally unaffected relative to wild-type mice.
L41: In a prenatal and postnatal development study, pregnant CD-1 mice received reslizumab during organogenesis on gestation days 6 and 18 and on postnatal day 14 at 10 or 50 mg/kg (1.5 and 6 times the exposures achieved at the MRHD on an AUC basis). Reslizumab did not have any effects on fetal development up to approximately 4 months after birth. Reslizumab crossed the placenta of pregnant mice. Serum concentrations in pups were approximately 6-8% of those in the dams (parental female mice) on postnatal day 14.
L42: ## 8.2 Lactation
L43: ## Risk Summary
L44: It is not known whether reslizumab is present in human milk, and the effects of reslizumab on the breast fed infant and on milk production are not known. However, human IgG is known to be present in human milk. Reslizumab was present in the milk of lactating mice following dosing during pregnancy [see Data]. The developmental and health benefits of breastfeeding should be considered along with the mother's clinical need for CINQAIR and any potential adverse effects on the breast-fed child from CINQAIR or the underlying maternal condition.
L45: ## Data
L46: Reslizumab was excreted in milk of lactating CD-1 mice that received reslizumab at 10 or 50 mg/kg (1.5 and 6 times the exposures achieved at the MRHD on an AUC basis) during pregnancy on gestation days 6 and 18 and on postnatal day 14. Levels of reslizumab in milk were approximately 5-7% of maternal serum concentrations.
L47: ## 10 OVERDOSAGE
L48: Single doses of up to (b)(4) mg have been administered intravenously to subjects (b)(4) without evidence of dose-related toxicities. There is no specific treatment for an overdose with CINQAIR. If overdose occurs, the patient should be treated supportively with appropriate monitoring as necessary.
L49: ## 11 DESCRIPTION
L50: CINQAIR (reslizumab) is a humanized interleukin-5 antagonist monoclonal antibody (IgG4\kappa). Reslizumab is produced by recombinant DNA technology in murine myeloma non-secreting 0 (NS0) cells. Reslizumab has a molecular weight of approximately 147 kDa.
L51: ## 12.1 Mechanism of Action
L52: Reslizumab is an interleukin-5 antagonist (IgG4, kappa). IL-5 is the major cytokine responsible for the growth and differentiation, recruitment, activation, and survival of eosinophils. Reslizumab binds to IL-5 with a dissociation constant of 81 pM, inhibiting the bioactivity of IL-5 by blocking its binding to the alpha chain of the IL-5 receptor complex expressed on the eosinophil surface. Inflammation is an important component in the pathogenesis of asthma. Multiple cell types (e.g., mast cells, eosinophils, neutrophils, macrophages, lymphocytes) and mediators (e.g., histamine, eicosanoids, leukotrienes, cytokines) are involved in inflammation. Reslizumab, by inhibiting IL-5 signaling, reduces the production and survival of eosinophils; however, the mechanism of reslizumab action in asthma has not been definitively established.
L53: ## 13 NONCLINICAL TOXICOLOGY
L54: ## 13.1 Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, Impairment of Fertility
L55: In a 6-month bioassay, reslizumab was administered intravenously once every two weeks for 26 consecutive weeks (14 total doses) to Tg.rasH2 mice at doses up to 516 mg/kg/dose. There was no evidence of carcinogenicity.
L56: Published literature using animal models suggests that IL-5 and eosinophils are part of an early inflammatory reaction at the site of tumorigenesis and can promote tumor rejection. However, other reports indicate that eosinophil infiltration into tumors can promote tumor growth. Therefore, the malignancy risk in humans from an antibody to IL-5 such as reslizumab is unknown.
L57: In a fertility study, administration of reslizumab to parental mice at doses up to 50 mg/kg (approximately 6 times the MRHD on an AUC basis) had no effects on male or female mating or fertility.
L58: ## 11 Integrated Summary and Safety Evaluation
L59: Teva submitted revised labeling for CINQAIR (reslizumab) Injection on January 4, 2016, per the Division's request during a teleconference held on December 14, 2015. The content of the nonclinical sections in the revised prescribing information [sections 8.1 "Pregnancy", 8.2 "Lactation", (b) (4) 10 "Overdosage", 11 "Description" (first paragraph only), 12.1 "Mechanism of Action", and 13 "Nonclinical Toxicology"] was evaluated. The approved label for NUCALA (mepolizumab) was used for reference as this is the first approved drug of the same pharmacologic class (i.e., humanized interleukin-5 antagonist monoclonal antibody).
L60: The prescribing information was submitted using the new Pregnancy and Lactation Labeling Rule (PLLR) format; therefore, a review was also conducted by the Division of Pediatric and Maternal Health (DPMH). Final language for sections 8.1 "Pregnancy" and 8.2 "Lactation" is based on agreement between DPMH and DPARP and is consistent with current thinking.
L61: Below is the proposed labeling text with recommended edits. Deleted text is shown as strikethrough text and inserted text is shown in red. An explanation for the proposed changes is included in *italics* under "Rationale for Changes".

L78: ## **Rationale for Changes in section 8.1 “Pregnancy”**
L79: The proposed edits under “Risk Summary” include recommendations made by the DPMH and are based on the PLLR and current thinking. For the animal data, the exposure ratios were changed (b) (4) to AUC because there are adequate AUC data in CD-1 mice. In addition, the reproductive and developmental toxicity studies were revised to include specific details on the timing of dosing in these studies. Further, information regarding embryo-fetal development in IL-5 deficient mice was included as this is public information relevant to this label and to be consistent with the approved label for mepolizumab (NUCALA).

L124: This consult review includes a nonclinical safety evaluation of potential extractables in Cinqair (reslizumab) injection. Dr. Ramesh Potla, OBP reviewer, submitted a request via email on February 3, 2016 (Attachment 1) to seek input on the adequacy of an extractables risk assessment conducted by Teva Branded Pharmaceutical Products R&amp;D, Inc. (BLA applicant)
L125: (b) (4) submitted a quarterly report to the FDA as part of a response to an FDA Form 483 observation made during the site's inspection in (b) (4). According to the report, (b) (4) committed to perform leachables/extractables studies (b) (4) in the manufacture of various drug substances, including reslizumab. (b) (4) provided an "Extractables Validation Report" (report #15-3723-SPTC-KVGLG) along with a risk assessment conducted by Teva for all the extractables identified during testing. The extractables testing (b) (4) was conducted using identical to worst-case conditions (time, temperature, and repeated cycles) and (b) (4) modeled after the actual pharmaceutical formulation. Table 1 below includes a list of extractable substances identified during testing. Reslizumab drug substance is packaged in (b) (4)
L126: **Table 1: List of Extractable Substances Identified**
L127: (b) (4)
L128: The safety of each of these extractables was determined by calculating their potential maximum daily dose in humans. The estimates are based on a maximum proposed clinical dose of 3 mg/kg reslizumab every 4 weeks (180 mg/dose for a 60 kg individual). Reslizumab drug product is manufactured in a vial at a concentration of 10 mg/mL. Each vial contains 10 mL of drug product. Therefore, the potential maximum dose of each extractable substance is approximately \text{[REDACTED]}^{(b)(4)} \mu\text{g}/\text{dose} (\text{[REDACTED]}^{(b)(4)} \mu\text{g} every 4 weeks). Table 2 below includes an example for one of the extractable substances that is present at \text{[REDACTED]}^{(b)(4)} mg (the highest amount detected for a single substance \text{[REDACTED]}^{(b)(4)}).
L129: **Table 2: Potential Maximum Dose of Extractables in Humans.**
L130: (b) (4)
L131: Teva concluded that the total amount of each extractable (\mu\text{g}/\text{dose}) is below the Safety Concern Threshold (SCT) of \mu\text{g}/\text{day} (defined by Teva as the threshold in which a leachable/extractable would have a dose so low as to present negligible safety concern from carcinogenic and non-carcinogenic effects) and therefore, it is unlikely that exposure to the extractables will adversely impact patient safety.
L132: After review of all the relevant information, this reviewer considers that Teva's approach to assess the risk of these extractables is adequate and agrees that there are no safety concerns for the potential maximum levels of these extractables in humans.

L137: ## Secondary Pharmacology and Toxicology Review for BLA 761-033
L138: TO: BLA 761-033 (Teva Branded Pharmaceutical Products R&amp;D, Inc.)

FROM: Marcie Wood, Ph.D.
Supervisory Pharmacologist
Division of Pulmonary, Allergy, and Rheumatology Products

DATE: December 23, 2015
L139: **Overview:** I concur with the recommendation of Dr. Carol Galvis (detailed in a nonclinical review dated December 16, 2015) that the pharmacology and toxicology of Cinqair (reslizumab) have been adequately studied and the drug product should be approved from a nonclinical perspective.
L140: **Background:** Reslizumab (Code names: CEP-38072, CTx55700, SCH 55700) is a humanized monoclonal antibody (IgG4 kappa), specific for interleukin-5 (IL-5). The applicant's proposed indication is to reduce exacerbations, relieve symptoms, and improve lung function in adults and adolescents (12 years of age and above) with asthma and elevated blood eosinophils who are inadequately controlled on inhaled corticosteroids. The recommended dose of reslizumab is 3 mg/kg administered once every 4 weeks by IV injection.
L141: **Pharmacology:** Reslizumab is a humanized monoclonal antibody (IgG4 kappa) that targets human IL-5. IL-5 is the major cytokine responsible for the growth and differentiation, recruitment, activation, and survival of eosinophils. The pharmacology of reslizumab was evaluated both *in vitro* and *in vivo*. *In vitro*, reslizumab binds with similar affinity to human, monkey, and mouse IL-5 (K_D = 24, 20, and 31 pM, respectively). Reslizumab also inhibited human IL-5 stimulation of TF-1 cells with an IC_{50} of 45 pM. *In vivo*, reslizumab was evaluated in animal models of eosinophilia, including mouse, monkey, rabbit, and guinea pig. Treatment with reslizumab decreased pulmonary eosinophilia after ovalbumin challenge in mice and guinea pigs, as well as in *Ascaris*-challenged monkeys. Reslizumab also reduced eosinophil influx into the skin of antigen challenged New Zealand rabbits.
L142: **Toxicology:** The nonclinical general toxicology program with reslizumab was conducted in CD-1 mice and Cynomolgus monkeys, both of which were determined to be pharmacologically relevant test species. In a chronic 6-month toxicology study in CD-1 mice that received reslizumab by the IV route at doses up to 25 mg/kg once every 4 weeks, eosinophil counts were generally lower in reslizumab treated animals versus controls, though the decreases were not statistically significant. There were no other reslizumab-related findings in this study. The NOAEL of 25 mg/kg q4 weeks provides adequate safety margin (3-fold on an AUC basis) for the proposed clinical dose of 3 mg/kg IV q4 weeks.
L143: In a chronic 6-month toxicology study in Cynomolgus monkeys that received reslizumab by the IV route at doses up to 25 mg/kg once every 4 weeks, no reslizumab-related findings were observed, and the NOAEL was identified as 25 mg/kg q4 weeks. This NOAEL also provides adequate safety margin (3-fold on an AUC basis) for the proposed clinical dose of 3 mg/kg IV q4 weeks.
L144: **Carcinogenicity:** A 6-month carcinogenicity study was conducted in HRAS transgenic mice, a confirmed pharmacologically relevant mouse strain. In this study, mice received 100, 250, or 500 mg/kg reslizumab IV once every two weeks. No statistically significant, drug-related neoplastic findings were observed in males or females.
L145: **Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology:** Reproductive and developmental toxicology studies were conducted in CD-1 mice (fertility, embryo-fetal development, and pre-natal/post-natal development) and New Zealand rabbits (embryo-fetal development). There were no drug-related effects on female or male fertility, teratogenicity, or pre-natal and post-natal development.
L146: **Labeling:** Nonclinical recommendations for reslizumab labeling are currently being developed, and Dr. Galvis will address reslizumab labeling in a separate review.
L147: There are no outstanding Pharmacology and Toxicology issues for this product.
L148: This is a representation of an electronic record that was signed electronically and this page is the manifestation of the electronic signature.
L149: /s/
L150: MARCIE L WOOD

L163: ## 1 Executive Summary
L164: ## 1.1 Introduction
L165: This review evaluates nonclinical pharmacology and toxicology data to support the safety of reslizumab. Teva Branded Pharmaceutical Products R&amp;D, Inc. submitted a Biologics License Application (BLA) on March 29, 2015, for Cinqair (reslizumab) injection. Reslizumab is a humanized monoclonal antibody (IgG4/k isotype) that targets interleukin-5 (IL-5) and is proposed to be marketed to reduce exacerbations, relieve symptoms and improve lung function in patients with asthma and elevated blood eosinophils who are inadequately controlled on inhaled corticosteroids. Reslizumab was developed for eosinophilic asthma under IND 101,399.
L166: ## 1.2 Brief Discussion of Nonclinical Findings
L167: The nonclinical program for reslizumab included studies conducted in mice, rabbits, and monkeys, which were all considered pharmacologically relevant species based on data from in vivo eosinophilia models in sensitized animals (reslizumab administration significantly reduced eosinophils in these animals). Reslizumab binds with similar affinity to human, monkey, and mouse IL-5 (K_D = 24, 20, and 31 pM; respectively, as determined by BIAcore biosensor assays).
L168: In a 6-month toxicology study, CD-1 mice were treated with 2, 10, or 25 mg/kg reslizumab IV once every 28 days. There were no drug-related findings observed in this study. Eosinophil counts were generally lower in treated animals compared to the vehicle control. However, the change was not statistically significant. Reslizumab systemic exposure in mice decreased over time, suggesting the presence of neutralizing anti-reslizumab antibodies (ADA). However, the ADA test was affected by the presence of free drug in serum.
L169: In a 6-month toxicology study, cynomolgus monkeys were treated with 1, 5, or 25 mg/kg reslizumab IV once every 28 days. No drug-related toxicity findings were observed in this study. A few animals in this study developed ADA. However, reslizumab exposure was confirmed in an adequate number of animals to assess the toxicity parameters.
L170: Reproductive and developmental toxicology studies were conducted in CD-1 mice (fertility, embryo-fetal development, and pre-natal/post-natal development) and New Zealand rabbits (embryo-fetal development). There were no drug-related effects on female or male fertility, teratogenicity, or pre-natal and post-natal development.
L171: Because the mouse was identified as a relevant species for reslizumab, the sponsor conducted a 6-month carcinogenicity study using HRAS transgenic mice (which was confirmed to be a pharmacologically relevant mouse strain). In the 26-week study, mice received 100, 250, or 500 mg/kg reslizumab IV once every two weeks. No statistically significant neoplastic findings were observed in males or females.
L172: ## 1.3 Recommendations
L173: ## 1.3.1 Applicability
L174: The nonclinical safety program for reslizumab is considered complete and adequate to support safety of the proposed clinical dose of 3 mg/kg every 4 weeks. BLA 761033 is recommended for approval from the nonclinical perspective.
L175: ## 1.3.2 Additional Non Clinical Recommendations
L176: None.
L177: ## 1.3.3 Labeling
L178: A labeling review will be conducted separately.

L192: ## 2.3 Drug Formulation
L193: Cinqair (reslizumab) is supplied as a refrigerated, sterile, single-use, preservative-free solution for intravenous infusion. Cinqair is a clear to slightly hazy/opalescent, colorless to slightly yellow liquid. Since reslizumab is a protein, proteinaceous particles may be present in the solution that appear as translucent to white, amorphous particulates, some of which may have a fibrous nature. Cinqair is supplied as 100 mg in a 10 mL glass vial. Each single vial is formulated as 10 mg/mL reslizumab in an aqueous solution containing 2.45 mg/mL sodium acetate trihydrate, 0.12 mg/mL glacial acetic acid, and 70 mg/mL sucrose, with a pH = 5.5. Cinqair is diluted with saline prior to infusion.
L194: Cinqair solution for infusion is packaged in a Type I clear, borosilicate glass vial, stoppered with a (b) (4) rubber serum stopper and sealed with an aluminum flip-off seal.
L195: The drug product composition and the function of each of the components are listed in Table 1 below (excerpted from the sponsor's submission).

L201: ## 2.5 Comments on Impurities/Degradants of Concern
L202: There were no impurities or degradants issues that impacted the nonclinical safety program. For any additional issues with drug substance or drug product impurities, refer to the Drug Quality review.
L203: ## 2.6 Proposed Clinical Population and Dosing Regimen
L204: Cinqair is proposed to be indicated to reduce exacerbations, relieve symptoms, and improve lung function in adults and adolescents (12 years of age and older) with asthma and elevated blood eosinophils who are inadequately controlled on inhaled corticosteroids. Cinqair is not indicated for acute bronchospasm.
L205: Cinqair is for intravenous (IV) infusion only. The recommended dosing regimen is 3 mg/kg administered once every 4 weeks.
L206: ## 2.7 Regulatory Background
L207: Reslizumab was originally developed for asthma under BB-IND 009,227 (Schering Corporation); which was submitted on August 1, 2000, and subsequently withdrawn on November 6, 2003. On January 16, 2008, two INDs were submitted simultaneously: IND 101,399 was submitted to DPARP to develop reslizumab for the treatment of eosinophilic asthma

L225: ## 4 Pharmacology
L226: ## 4.1 Primary Pharmacology
L227: ## Brief summary
L228: Reslizumab is a humanized IL-5 antagonist (monoclonal antibody of the IgG4/\kappa isotype) that contains the complementarity determining regions (CDRs) of the rat anti-human antibody 39D10 grafted onto human frameworks. Reslizumab binds to IL-5 and prevents its binding to the IL-5 receptor, thereby reducing circulating and tissue eosinophils. Reslizumab binds with similar affinity to human, monkey, and mouse IL-5.
L229: Reslizumab has been tested in vivo in a number of species including mouse (multiple strains), monkey, rabbit, and guinea pig. Reslizumab was shown to inhibit eosinophilia in these species after intraperitoneal (IP) administration (mice and guinea pigs), intramuscular (IM) administration (mice), or intravenous (IV) administration (mice, monkeys, and rabbits).
L230: Most pharmacology studies were reviewed under INDs 101,399 (b) (4) to support development of reslizumab and only a summary is included herein. However, some studies were submitted to the INDs later and a more detailed review is included below.
L231: ## In vitro pharmacology
L232: Reslizumab has been tested in vitro using a number of methods, including a solid-phase antigen competition assay, a kinetic biosensor analysis of antigen-antibody interactions, an in vitro cell binding assay, and a cell proliferation assay. Table 2 below (excerpted from the sponsor's submission – “Pharmacology Written Summary”) includes a summary of the in vitro activity profile of reslizumab (CEP-38072) compared with the original rat anti-human antibody (39D10).
L233: **Table 2: In Vitro Activity Profile of Reslizumab.**
L234: Biotin-39D10 competitiona
(Relative IC50)	BIAcoreb
Kd (pM)	Receptor Bindingc
IC50(nM)	TF-1 Proliferationd
IC50 (nM)
CEP-38072	0.9	81	0.5	45
39D10	2	53	0.28	25
L235: SOURCE: Study D26740
L236: a Competition for binding to immobilized human IL-5 by ELISA.
L237: b BIAcore direct binding to human IL-5 immobilized on a sensor chip at 37°C.
L238: c Competitive inhibition of radiolabeled IL-5 binding to BaF3#5-8 cells expressing human IL-5 receptor.
L239: d Antagonism of IL-5 induced proliferation of human erythroleukemia TF-1 cells measured by MTT assay after ~44-48 hour incubation.
L240: IC50=half-maximal inhibitory concentration; Kd=dissociation constant.
L241: ## **Study # BDR-2014-045-BAR-WC: Surface Plasmon Resonance Analysis of Human, Monkey, and Mouse IL-5 Binding to Reslizumab.**
L242: This study was conducted to measure the affinity of reslizumab (CEP-38072, lot #12-000792) with human, monkey, and mouse IL-5 using a BIAcore T200 method. Briefly, anti-human Fc was immobilized to both the reference and active surfaces of a CM5 sensor chip surface using amine coupling. Reslizumab (1 µg/mL) was captured with a flow rate of 10 µL/min for 15-30 seconds onto the surface of the active flow cell. Binding to captured antibody was tested using 0.02, 0.07, 0.22, 0.67, and 2 nM IL-5 as the analyte.
L243: The results of this assay are summarized below in Table 3. Reslizumab shows a similar KD across the species tested (24 pM in human, 20 pM in monkey, and 31 pM in mouse).
L244: **Table 3: Kinetic Values for Reslizumab Binding to Human, Monkey, and Mouse IL-5.**
L245: Species	ka (1/Ms)	kd (1/s)	KD (pM)
Human	4.47x106	1.07x10-4	24
Monkey	4.04x106	8.14x10-5	20
Mouse	4.45x106	1.36 x10-4	31
L246: ka = association rate; kd = dissociation rate; KD = dissociation constant
L247: ## In vivo pharmacology
L248: Reslizumab has been tested in vivo in a number of models using multiple strains of mice, Hartley guinea pigs, New Zealand rabbits, and Cynomolgus monkeys. For the purposes of this review, only the studies in the nonclinical species used for toxicology assessment (CD-1 mouse, CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic mouse, New Zealand rabbits, and cynomolgus monkeys) will be described. Similar findings were observed in other species or strains. Results from these studies support the use of the CD-1 mouse, the CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic transgenic mouse, the New Zealand rabbit, and the cynomolgus monkey in the nonclinical pharmacology and toxicology studies discussed herein.
L249: ## *CD-1 Mouse*
L250: ## **Study #D-27201: Inhibition of Allergic Pulmonary Eosinophilia by SCH55700 in CD-1 Mice.**
L251: This study was conducted to support the use of CD-1 mouse strain for toxicology assessment of reslizumab. Briefly, male CD-1 mice (5-6/group) were sensitized with ovalbumin (OVA, IP injections) and then received OVA booster injections 5 days later. Then animals were exposed to aerosolized OVA (0.5%) for 1 hour in the morning and 1 hour in the afternoon. Reslizumab (SCH55700) was administered by IP injection at 1 or 10 mg/kg 2 hours prior to the aerosolized OVA challenge. At selected times after OVA challenge, mice were sacrificed by CO2 asphyxiation, the trachea was exposed and cannulated, and lungs were lavaged. Bronchioalveolar lavage (BAL) fluid was collected for differential cell count, pulmonary tissue was collected for histological evaluation to evaluate the number of eosinophils surrounding the bronchi and bronchioles, and blood was collected from these animals to measure serum IgE.
L252: Results from this study show that reslizumab inhibited the development of pulmonary eosinophilia after OVA antigen challenge, measured as a statistically significant decrease in eosinophils in BAL fluid, bronchi, and bronchioles (refer to Table 4 below, excerpted from the study report).
L253: **Table 4: Effect of Reslizumab (SCH55700) on Eosinophil Accumulation in the BAL Fluid and Lung Tissue of Allergic CD-1 Mice.**
L254: Sensitization	Sch 55700 (mg/kg, i.p.)	NUMBER OF EOSINOPHILSa
BAL Fluid (\times 10^3/ml)	Bronchi (#/high power field)	Bronchioles (#/high power field)
-	-	0 \pm 0^*	1 \pm 1^*	0 \pm 0^*
+	-	245 \pm 72	43 \pm 3	19 \pm 3
+	1	96 \pm 29^*	28 \pm 3^*	11 \pm 3^*
+	10	53 \pm 18^*	19 \pm 5^*	4 \pm 1^*
L255: a Mean \pm SEM (n = 10-12 per dose) number of eosinophils measured 48 hr after OVA challenge.
L256: * P &lt; 0.05 compared with the sensitized, challenged group that did not receive Sch 55700.
L257: The data are found in Notebook #34190 pages 149-160 and 290-305.
L258: ## *CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic Mouse*
L259: **Study #DRR-2013-001, "Effect of IV Delivered CEP-38072 in OVA-Sensitized CByB6F1-Tg (HRAS)2jic Mice".** This study was reviewed previously to support the use of this transgenic mouse strain for the 26-week carcinogenicity study. Refer to PharmTox memo dated February 11, 2014. The purpose of this study was to test reslizumab in an OVA model using CByB6F1-Tg (HRAS)2jic mice to determine if reslizumab had an effect on allergic airway responses. Briefly, mice (10 males/group) were challenged with ovalbumin (OVA, 20 \mug IP) to create an allergic response [i.e., increase in inflammatory cells in bronchioalveolar lavage (BAL)]. OVA-sensitized mice were treated with 10 mg/kg or 250 mg/kg reslizumab IV on study days 21 and 23. Three controls were included: an OVA-sensitized comparator group (treated with 3 mg/kg dexamethasone from study day 21 to study day 23), an OVA-sensitized negative control (treated with vehicle), and a non-sensitized negative control. Animals were euthanized 48-56 hours following the last OVA challenge on study day 23. Blood and BAL fluid were collected from all the animals. Results of this study show that reslizumab attenuates the inflammatory response in OVA-sensitized mice, shown as a statistically significant decrease in eosinophil levels in BAL fluid of mice treated with 250 mg/kg reslizumab IV.
L260: ## *New Zealand Rabbits*
L261: ## ***Study #D-27530: Bioactivity of SCH 55700 in Rabbits.***
L262: This study was conducted to assess the bioactivity of SCH 55700 (reslizumab) in rabbits to support the use of this nonclinical species in the embryo-fetal development study. A model of passive cutaneous anaphylaxis (PCA) was used to evaluate the effects of reslizumab on the influx of eosinophils into the skin of allergic rabbits in response to antigen challenge (ovalbumin or ragweed extract). Briefly, a group of 20
L263: rabbits was sensitized to ovalbumin (OVA) using 15 mg ovalbumin subcutaneously for a period of approximately 30 days. A separate group of 20 rabbits was sensitized to ragweed extract using 1 mL of 1:100 w/v ragweed extract/rabbit intraperitoneally for a period of approximately 14 days.
L264: After a period of approximately 7 days, IgE antibody titers were measured in the sensitized rabbits by homologous passive cutaneous anaphylaxis (PCA) to determine the animals that had been sensitized to ovalbumin or ragweed extract. Sensitized rabbits received either 5 mg/kg SCH 55700, 5 mg/kg human IgG, or saline intravenously (into an ear vein) two hours prior to antigen challenge. OVA-sensitized rabbits were then challenged with ovalbumin, saline, or bovine serum albumin injected intradermally. Ragweed-sensitized rabbits were challenged with ragweed extract or saline injected intradermally. After 24 hours, animals were sacrificed and skin biopsies were obtained. The total number of inflammatory cells, eosinophils, and neutrophils per unit area was calculated.
L265: Results of this study show that treatment with 5 mg/kg reslizumab 2 hours prior to OVA or ragweed extract challenge significantly reduced the number of eosinophils appearing in the skin of OVA- or ragweed-sensitized rabbits. This data supports the use of this nonclinical species for toxicology studies. Refer to Figure 2 below, excerpted from the sponsor's submission, for the effects of reslizumab treatment in OVA-sensitized rabbits. Results observed in ragweed-sensitized rabbits are not shown.
L266: **Figure 2: Effect of SCH 55700 on the Eosinophils in the Skin of Ovalbumin-Challenged Allergic Rabbits.**
L267: ° Unit area = 0.115 mm2
L268: *P&lt;0.05 compared to human IgG-treated group.
L269: ## *Cynomolgus Monkeys*
L270: ## **Study #D-26751: Effects of a Humanized Monoclonal Antibody to IL-5 (SCH55700) in a Monkey Model of Asthma.**
L271: This study evaluated the effect of reslizumab on pulmonary eosinophilia induced by *Ascaris* antigen challenge in wild-caught *Ascaris*-sensitive cynomolgus monkeys. Briefly, *Ascaris*-sensitive male monkeys (6/group) received 0.1 or 0.3 mg/kg reslizumab 1 hour before an aerosol challenge with *Ascaris* antigen. BAL fluid was collected for differential cell count immediately before and 24 hours after the *Ascaris* challenge. In addition, animals were instrumented to measure lung mechanics and an assessment of airway responsiveness to inhaled histamine was conducted.
L272: Results from this study are presented below in Figures 3 and 4, excerpted from the study report. As shown, 0.3 mg/kg reslizumab (SCH55700) treatment resulted in inhibition of pulmonary eosinophilia, measured as a statistically significant decrease in the number of eosinophils in BAL fluid collected from treated animals. No statistically significant effect was observed at 0.1 mg/kg reslizumab (Figure 4). According to the sponsor, the effect of reslizumab on airway hyper-reactivity could not be accurately assessed because there was a shift in baseline airway reactivity in control animals. The study report did not comment on whether or not reslizumab had an effect on lung mechanics.
L273: **Figure 3: Effect of SCH 55700 on the Pulmonary Eosinophilia Induced by *Ascaris* Challenge in Cynomolgus Monkeys – 0.3 mg/kg.**
L274: **Figure 4: Effect of SCH 55700 on the Pulmonary Eosinophilia Induced by *Ascaris* Challenge in Cynomolgus Monkeys – 0.1 mg/kg.**
L275: ## 4.2 Secondary Pharmacology
L276: For secondary pharmacology, the sponsor used a mouse model of allergic conjunctivitis (study #DRR-2013-006). In this model, mice were sensitized with an antigen (ragweed), then challenged again with ragweed 17 days later and randomized to receive vehicle, reslizumab (1 mg/mL eye drops or IV), or prednisolone as a positive control. It is expected that the primary cell types involved in this model are basophils and mast cells. Therefore, the sponsor used this model to test whether reslizumab (which is proposed to specifically affect eosinophils) is effective. Results from this study were variable, but in general reslizumab did not have an effect in decreasing basophils or mast cells in this model.
L277: ## 4.3 Safety Pharmacology
L278: A full battery of safety pharmacology studies was conducted for reslizumab. However, many of these studies were conducted in the rat, which was not evaluated in vitro or in vivo to determine if it is a pharmacologically relevant species to test reslizumab. Only two single-dose studies (one toxicity study in CD rats and one PK study in Sprague-Dawley rats) were conducted in rats (refer to Section 6.1 below). Table 5 below presents a summary of the safety pharmacology studies conducted in rats.
L279: **Table 5: Summary of Safety Pharmacology Studies Conducted in Rats.**

L282: In addition to the above studies in rats, the sponsor also conducted study #P6670 in cynomolgus monkeys, a pharmacological relevant species. This study was conducted to assess CNS binding and cerebro-spinal fluid (CSF) levels of reslizumab following a single IV dose of 5 or 25 mg/kg. There were no drug-related effects observed in physical, neurologic, cardiovascular (ECG), or ophthalmic examinations. In addition, immunohistochemical staining did not show reslizumab binding to CNS tissue. Results from CSF analysis showed the presence of low levels of reslizumab (&lt;0.1 µg/mL) in these samples.
L283: An additional study (#SN99222) was conducted in cynomolgus monkeys to further evaluate the potential effects of reslizumab in the CNS. In this study, reslizumab was administered as a single intrathecal injection (0 or 0.07 mg/kg) into the cisterna magna. This route was used in order to achieve CSF concentrations that were higher than the IV concentrations. Organ weight measurements and gross and microscopic examinations were performed on brain, eyes with optic nerve, spinal cord, and trigeminal nerve. In this study, no drug-related effects were observed in any of the aforementioned parameters. Reslizumab concentration in CSF ranged from approximately 82,000-111,000 ng/mL (individual animal values) at the time points assessed.
L284: Further, no findings of concern were observed (behavior, cardiovascular, or respiratory rate) in the pivotal toxicity studies conducted in mice and monkeys and discussed below in Section 6.2
L285: ## 5 Pharmacokinetics/ADME/Toxicokinetics

L287: ## Brief summary
L288: A number of pharmacokinetic (PK) studies were conducted with reslizumab in CD-1 mouse, Sprague-Dawley rat, New Zealand rabbit, and cynomolgus monkey. A
L289: summary of study designs and noteworthy findings is included in Table 7 below under Section 6.1. Table 6 (excerpted from the sponsor's submission) includes a summary of serum PK parameters for intravenous reslizumab across nonclinical species.

L294: PK parameters were generally dose-proportional for all nonclinical species across a range of doses. There was evidence of anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) development in all nonclinical species tested. Analysis of PK parameters in ADA-positive animals found that the antibodies were neutralizing, as reslizumab was not detected in the blood of these animals.
L295: There were no distribution, metabolism, or excretion studies conducted with reslizumab. As expected for a monoclonal antibody, reslizumab had low systemic clearance, low volume of distribution, and a long terminal half-life across nonclinical species.
L296: Reslizumab was excreted in the milk of lactating mice and there was placental transfer from dams to pups. Refer to Section 9.3 below for additional details.
L297: ## 5.2 Toxicokinetics
L298: The toxicokinetics (TK) parameters of reslizumab are discussed below in Section 6, under each of the studies discussed.

L306: ## 6.2 Repeat-Dose Toxicity
L307: Repeat-dose GLP toxicology studies were conducted in CD-1 mice, CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic mice, and cynomolgus monkeys using the IV route of administration. These general toxicity studies were reviewed previously to support the use of reslizumab in clinical trials (refer to Appendices 1-3) and only a summary of the study design and noteworthy findings is included below.
L308: In addition, juvenile toxicity studies were conducted in monkeys and mice. These studies were reviewed in detail for the purposes of this review. A summary of the toxicology findings by nonclinical species/strains is included below.
L309: ## Mouse
L310: ## *CD-1 Mouse*
L311: Repeat-dose toxicity studies (4-week and 6-month studies) were conducted in CD-1 mice, a pharmacological relevant species for reslizumab, using the IV route of administration. A summary of these studies follows.
L312: **Study #P6190, One-Month (Two-Dose) Intravenous Toxicity of SCH55700 (Anti-IL-5) in Mice.** Briefly, male and female CD-1 mice (20/sex/group) received 0, 1, 5, or 25 mg/kg reslizumab IV on study days 1 and 15. At the end of the dosing period, animals were sacrificed and necropsies were performed. Assessment of toxicity was based on mortality, clinical signs, body weights, food consumption, and clinical and anatomic pathology observations. In addition, a satellite group was included for TK and ADA analysis. Necropsies were conducted at study weeks 5 (10 animals/sex/group) and 9 (10 animals/sex/group). No drug-related findings were observed at any dose level in this study and the NOAEL was identified at the 25 mg/kg dose (C_{max} = 354 µg/mL, AUC0-14days = 83,837 µg.hr/mL in males and females combined).
L313: **Study #SN96264, Six-Month Intravenous Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study of SCH55700 in Mice.** Briefly, male and female CD-1 mice (10 mice/sex/group) were treated with 0, 2, 10, or 25 mg/kg SCH 55700 (reslizumab) IV once every 28 days for 6 months (7 doses total). At the end of the dosing period, animals were euthanized and necropsies were performed. Assessment of toxicity was based on mortality, clinical signs, body weights, food consumption, ophthalmic examinations, and clinical and anatomic pathology observations. In addition, blood samples were collected for toxicokinetic and antibody analysis. This study did not include a recovery period.
L314: Six animals died during the study: two animals in the LD group (undetermined reason), three animals in the MD group (two undetermined and one sacrificed due to a bronchiolo-alveolar carcinoma), and one animal in the HD group (animal 304, found dead due to urinary tract obstruction). The study report did not mention why the mortalities in the LD and MD groups were undetermined. Animal 304 (HD male) was found dead on the day of necropsy. Histopathology findings in this animal included moderate renal pelvis and ureters dilatation and mild congestion in the urinary bladder.
L315: Histopathological findings of concern in this study include renal pelvis and ureters dilatation in two additional HD male animals that correlated with elevated levels of blood urea-nitrogen (BUN), suggesting effects in the urinary tract in treated animals. At the Division's request, the sponsor provided an expert report that included re-examination of kidney tissues from all study animals and a toxicological evaluation of the renal findings. Because a clear dose-response was not observed, the expert report concludes that findings of renal pelvis dilatation are incidental and not related to treatment. This is supported by the fact that it was also observed in one control animal. In addition, literature search found that dilatation is a common incidental finding in CD-1 mice2. Therefore, it has been determined that these renal findings in the mouse are not related to treatment.
L316: TK parameters from this study show slightly decreased exposure with time, suggesting the presence of neutralizing antibodies. However, this was not confirmed because the ADA assay was affected by the presence of free reslizumab in serum. Eosinophil levels were measured in all animals and, although eosinophil levels trended lower in the treated groups compared to controls, the change was not statistically significant. The NOAEL for the kidney findings in the mouse is 25 mg/kg/dose (C_{max} = 412 µg/mL, AUC_{0-14days} = 45,500 µg.hr/mL in males and females combined).
L317: ## *CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic Mouse*
L318: A 4-week toxicity study was conducted in 001178-W (Wild Type) mice to support the special protocol assessment (SPA) for the 26-week carcinogenicity study in transgenic mice. A brief summary is provided below.
L319: **Study #DS-2011-017, 4-Week (Three Doses Total) Dose Range-Finding Intravenous Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study with CEP-38072 in 001178-W (Wild Type) Mice.** Briefly, mice (10/sex/group) were treated with 0 (vehicle), 250, and 500 mg/kg/dose reslizumab IV on study days 1, 15, and 29 (3 doses total). At the end of the dosing period, animals were sacrificed and necropsies were performed. Assessment of toxicity was based on mortality, clinical signs of toxicity, body weights, food consumption, and clinical and anatomic pathology observations. In addition, satellite groups were included for TK and ADA analysis. There were no drug-related toxicities observed in any of the parameters assessed. Therefore, the NOAEL was identified at 500 mg/kg/dose (AUC_{0-168 h} of 1326 mg*h/mL for females and 1519 mg.hr/mL for males).
L320: ## Cynomolgus Monkeys
L321: Repeat-dose toxicity studies (one-month and 6-month IV studies) were conducted in cynomolgus monkeys, a pharmacological relevant species for reslizumab. A summary of these studies follows.
L322: 2 Chandra M and Frith CH. *Spontaneous renal lesions in CD-1 and B6C3F1 mice.* (1994) Experimental Toxicology Pathology, Vol. 46, No. 3; pages 189-198.
L323: **Study #P6188, Two-Dose/ One-Month Intravenous Toxicity Study of SCH55700 (Anti-IL-5) in Cynomolgus Monkeys.** Briefly, male and female monkeys (4/sex/group) received 0, 1, 5, or 25 mg/kg reslizumab IV on study days 1 and 15. At the end of the dosing period, animals were sacrificed and necropsies were performed. Assessment of toxicity was based on mortality, clinical signs of toxicity, body weights, food consumption, physical examinations (including electrocardiograms, body temperature, respiratory rates, heart rates and blood pressure), ophthalmic examinations and clinical and anatomic pathology observations. In addition, blood samples were collected for TK and ADA analysis. There were no drug-related effects observed at any dose level in this study. Therefore, the NOAEL was identified at the 25 mg/kg dose (mean serum reslizumab concentration = 129,716 ng/mL).
L324: **Study #P6749, A Six-Month Intravenous Toxicity Study of SCH55700 with Neurological Evaluations in Cynomolgus Monkeys.** Briefly, cynomolgus monkeys (4/sex/group) were treated with 0, 1, 5, or 25 mg/kg SCH 55700 IV once every 4 weeks for 6 months (7 doses). At the end of the dosing period, animals were euthanized and necropsies were performed. Two animals in the control and HD groups underwent a 13 week treatment-free phase. Assessment of toxicity was based on mortality, clinical signs, body weights, food consumption, ophthalmic and ECG examinations, and clinical and anatomic pathology observations. In addition, blood samples were collected for toxicokinetic and antibody (ADA) analysis.
L325: No significant SCH 55700-related effects were observed on mortality, clinical signs, body weights, food consumption, ophthalmic examinations, clinical pathology, or neurological and electroneurological examinations. ECG examinations found that one MD male showed a single ventricular extrasystole at week 28, one HD female had two ventricular extrasystoles at week 8 and one HD male showed a single ventricular extrasystole at weeks 16 and 28, and three at week 22.
L326: Histopathology findings include injection site vasculitis observed in 3 HD males but this was not considered dose-limiting. In addition, kidney findings with increased incidence in HD animals include tubular basophilia (4 HD M), and hypoplasia (1 HD F). The sponsor was not able to provide additional information to support that these findings in kidney were not related to treatment. Therefore, they proposed to examine kidney tissues
L327: (b) (4)
L328: ## Juvenile Toxicity
L329: The sponsor assessed reslizumab's toxicity in juvenile animals (mice and monkeys). The juvenile toxicity in CD-1 mouse was conducted as part of the peri- and post-natal reproduction and development study (#DS-2010-020) included below under Section 9.3 and, therefore, the review is also included under Section 9.3. The juvenile toxicity in monkeys was assessed in a standalone study (study #Res-4-0006) that is reviewed below.
L330: ## **Study title: A One-Month (Four Once Weekly Doses) Intravenous Bridging Toxicity Study of Reslizumab in Juvenile Monkeys, with a One-Month Recovery Period and Pharmacokinetic and Anti-Reslizumab Antibody Evaluations.**
L331: Study no.:	Res-4-0006
Study report location:	EDR
Conducting laboratory and location:	(b) (4)
Date of study initiation:	September 6, 2007
GLP compliance:	Yes
QA statement:	Yes
Drug, lot #, and % purity:	Reslizumab, lots# 00U-557P-00003 (purity not specified) and #52758 (99.9% pure)
L332: ## **Key Study Findings**
L333: - Juvenile monkeys (1.4-2.3 years old) were treated with 1 mg/kg (two different reslizumab drug lots, #00U-557P-00003 and #52758) or 15 mg/kg reslizumab intravenously once weekly for 4 consecutive weeks.
- The toxicity profiles and PK parameters were similar for the two drug lots tested from two different manufacturers, supporting bridging for these two drug lots.
- A few histological findings were observed: multifocal hemorrhage in GALT in the rectum of one HD male, bilateral fibrosis in testis of 3/8 LD (37.5%) and 2/4 HD (50%) males, lymphoid depletion in the thymus of 3/16 LD animals and 2/8 HD animals, and menses in 2/4 HD females; but these were not considered related to treatment.
- A few females showed menses during the study, indicating that some of the animals used in the study were sexually mature.
- There was high variability in PK parameters calculated from samples from the 15 mg/kg group. PK parameters in this study increased in a higher than dose-proportional manner from 1 mg/kg to 15 mg/kg. There was no gender difference in exposure. Anti-drug antibodies were not detected in any of the blood samples tested.
- The NOAEL was identified at 15 mg/kg/dose (C_{max} = 774.36 \mug/mL; AUC_{ss} = 63,670 \mug\cdothr/mL).

Species/Strain:	Cynomolgus monkeys from (b) (4)

2/sex/group (recovery)

Females: 1.6-2.6 kg
Satellite groups:	Not applicable
Unique study design:	Four animals/sex/group were euthanized on study day 29 and the remaining 2 animals/sex/group were maintained for a recovery period of 4 weeks (refer to Table 8 below, excerpted from the study report).
Deviation from study protocol:	Protocol deviations were reviewed and judged not to affect the integrity of the study data.
L336: The objectives of this study were to evaluate the potential toxicity of reslizumab in juvenile monkeys to support clinical studies in children with eosinophilic disease and to compare the toxicity profile of two manufactured lots of reslizumab [SCH55700 by Schering Corporation (previous sponsor) and CTx55700 by [REDACTED](b) (4) on behalf of Ception Therapeutics] to justify the use of toxicity data from previous studies in monkeys using an older lot. The study examined weekly doses of 1 and 15 mg/kg intravenous reslizumab.

L340: ## Observations and Results
L341: ## Mortality
L342: Animals were observed for signs of mortality and/or morbidity twice daily in the morning and in the afternoon beginning 5 days prior to dosing and continuing through the end of the dosing phase.
L343: No drug-related findings were observed.
L344: ## Clinical Signs
L345: Cage-side observations were conducted once daily in the morning. Animals were observed for changes in general appearance and behavior.
L346: No drug-related findings were observed. A few females were observed with hemorrhage (menses) and this was confirmed by histopathology. This is not considered related to treatment.
L347: ## Body Weights
L348: Body weights were measured at least twice prior to the first dose and weekly thereafter. Food was withheld prior to body weight measurements.
L349: No drug-related findings were observed.
L350: ## Feed Consumption
L351: The number of biscuits remaining from the previous days feeding was observed and recorded every morning. A notation of low food consumption was made when less than approximately half of the rations were consumed.
L352: No drug-related findings were observed.
L353: ## Ophthalmoscopy
L354: Not conducted.
L355: ## ECG
L356: Not conducted.
L357: ## Hematology
L358: Blood samples for clinical pathology were collected from all animals prior to initiation of dosing at study week -1, during the week following the final dose (study week 4) and near the end of the recovery period (study week 8). Samples were collected into tubes containing EDTA as anticoagulant. The following hematology parameters were evaluated: red blood cell count, hemoglobin concentration, hematocrit, mean corpuscular volume, mean corpuscular hemoglobin concentration, mean corpuscular hemoglobin, reticulocyte count, platelet count, white blood cell count, and blood cell morphology.
L359: Table 9 below presents a summary of hematology changes relative to controls. As presented, eosinophil counts were decreased in all animals in a dose-dependent manner. Lymphocytes and unclassified cells were decreased in animals treated with 15 mg/kg reslizumab. Also, segmented neutrophils were increased in all groups relative to controls.

L364: ## Clinical Chemistry
L365: Blood samples for clinical pathology were collected from all animals from the femoral vein prior to initiation of dosing at study week -1, during the week following the final dose (study week 4) and near the end of the recovery period (study week 8). The animals were fasted for at least 8 hours prior to blood collection for serum chemistry analysis. A full battery of serum chemistry parameters was evaluated.
L366: No drug-related findings were observed.
L367: ## Urinalysis
L368: Samples for urinalysis were collected at necropsy by cystocentesis. A full battery of urinalysis parameters was evaluated.
L369: No drug-related findings were observed.
L370: ## Gross Pathology
L371: On study day 29, animals in the main study were sacrificed by exsanguination while under deer anesthesia induced with ketamine and Beuthanasia-D. Animals continuing through the recovery period had blood collected from the vena cava while under Nembutal anesthesia. Following blood collection, animals were sacrificed using Nembutal injection and exsanguination. A terminal body weight was obtained at necropsy.
L372: All animals were subjected to a complete gross necropsy examination that included evaluation of the carcass and musculoskeletal system; all external surfaces and orifices; cranial cavity and external surfaces of the brain; and thoracic, abdominal, and pelvic cavities with their associated organs and tissues.
L373: No drug-related findings were observed.
L374: ## Organ Weights
L375: For all euthanasias, the following organs were weighed before fixation: adrenals, brain, epididymides, heart, kidneys, liver, lungs, ovaries, pituitary, spleen, testes, thymus, prostate gland, thyroid with parathyroid, uterus (plus cervix), and salivary glands (mandibular, sublingual, and parotid). Paired organs were weighed together unless gross abnormalities were present, in which case they were weighed separately.
L376: A few changes in organ weights were observed and are summarized below in Table 10. Spleen weight was decreased in HD males but not in females, and thyroid weights were decreased in both groups treated with the new drug product CTx55700 but not with the old product from Schering (SCH55700). It is unclear whether this finding is due to differences in the two products. In addition, pituitary weights were decreased in HD females but not males. In HD females, ovaries and uterus weights were increased and this could be associated with the menses observed upon histopathological examination.

L381: ## Histopathology
L382: ## Adequate Battery
L383: A full battery of tissues was collected at necropsy from all euthanized animals. In addition, a bone marrow smear was collected from the seventh rib of each animal at necropsy, stained, and stored. The smears were not examined microscopically at necropsy as a drug-related alteration in bone marrow sections was not observed.
L384: ## Peer Review
L385: A peer review was not conducted for this study.
L386: ## Histological Findings
L387: A summary of histological findings is presented below in Table 11. The following findings were observed in drug-treated animals: multifocal hemorrhage in GALT in the rectum of one HD male, bilateral fibrosis in testis of 3/8 LD (37.5%) and 2/4 HD (50%) males, lymphoid depletion in the thymus of 3/16 LD animals and 2/8 HD animals, and menses in 2/4 HD females. The findings in lymphoid tissues may be considered related to treatment but are not dose-limiting. It is not clear whether the findings in testis are related to treatment. Even though there appears to be a dose-response, one control recovery male had a similar finding with the same severity, which alleviates the concern. In females, menses was observed in 2 HD animals and 1 LD recovery animal, which indicates that some of the females included in the study are sexually mature.
L388: Because none of the aforementioned findings are considered related to treatment, the NOAEL was identified in this study at 15 mg/kg/dose, which is associated with an AUCss of 63,670 µg·hr/mL (males and females combined at study week 4).

L396: ## Special Evaluation
L397: Blood samples were collected from all available animals for anti-drug antibody (ADA) analysis prior to the first dose on study day 1, prior to the last dose on study day 22, and on study day 29. Samples were also collected from recovery animals on study day 57. A validated ELISA method was used for detection of ADA in the samples.
L398: TK and immunogenicity results were submitted to the BLA in a separate report #Res-4-0009 entitled "Pharmacokinetic Report: A one-month (four once weekly doses) intravenous bridging toxicity study of reslizumab in juvenile cynomolgus monkeys, with a one-month recovery period and pharmacokinetic and anti-reslizumab antibody evaluations". The study report for study #Res-4-0006 has a GLP exception regarding this. None of the samples tested (160 samples from 48 animals across groups) were positive for ADA in this study.
L399: ## Toxicokinetics
L400: Blood samples were collected from the femoral vein by venipuncture for TK analysis prior to each dose on study days 1, 8, 15, and 22. Additional samples were collected from all animals following the final dose on study day 22 at 15 minutes, 1 hour, 4 hours, 8 hours, and 24 hours post-dose. Samples were evaluated for reslizumab
L401: concentrations using a validated enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA) method. Analysis of pharmacokinetic parameters was conducted for each animal and dose groups were summarized.
L402: TK and immunogenicity results were submitted to the BLA in study #Res-4-0009. The study report for study #Res-4-0006 has a GLP exception regarding this. Table 12 below, excerpted from the study report, shows that the two drug products from two different manufacturers had similar PK profiles. In addition, there was no gender difference in PK parameters.

L409: ## Dosing Solution Analysis
L410: Dosing formulations were analyzed for reslizumab concentration using an ELISA method. In addition, the dosing formulations were analyzed for stability and considered to be stable under the conditions of the study.
L411: Results of dosing formulation analysis were not included in the study report. According to the report, under GLP exceptions, the sponsor was in charge of analyzing the dosing formulations for concentration and homogeneity. The results were provided to the study director but not included in the final report.
L412: ## 7 Genetic Toxicology
L413: Even though reslizumab is a large molecule (a monoclonal antibody) and is not expected to interact with DNA, the sponsor conducted two in vitro genetic toxicology assays, a bacterial reverse mutation (Ames) assay, and an in vitro chromosomal aberration assay. The results of both in vitro assays were negative. Refer to PharmTox review under IND 101,399 dated February 19, 2008 (appendix 1), for additional details.
L414: ## 8 Carcinogenicity
L415: The applicant conducted a 26-week study in Tg.rasH2 mice to assess the carcinogenic potential of reslizumab. The study protocol was discussed with the ECAC in a meeting held on July 10, 2012, and was considered adequate. In addition, the sponsor also provided data from a bioassay (statistically significant decrease in eosinophil levels in BAL fluid of OVA-sensitized mice treated with 250 mg/kg reslizumab) demonstrating that the transgenic mouse strain was pharmacologically relevant (refer to Section 4 "Pharmacology" above).
L416: In the 26-week carcinogenicity study #DS-2012-005, Tg.rasH2 mice (25/sex/group) received 0, 100, 250, or 500 mg/kg reslizumab IV once every two weeks for 26 weeks. A positive control group receiving 75 mg/kg MNU intraperitoneally was also included for comparison. There were no statistically significant neoplastic findings observed in males or females in this study. Refer to PharmTox review under BLA 761033 dated June 30, 2015 for complete details.
L417: ## 9 Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L418: A battery of reproductive and developmental toxicology studies was conducted with reslizumab. These studies evaluated the effects of reslizumab on fertility in CD-1 mice (study # P-6981), teratogenicity in CD-1 mice and New Zealand white rabbits (studies #P-6378 and #P-6379, respectively), and peri- and post-natal development and juvenile toxicity in CD-1 mice (study #DS-2010-020). A detailed review of these studies is included below.
L419: The sponsor conducted a number of studies to support the use of the CD-1 mouse and New Zealand rabbit as nonclinical species for assessment of reproductive and developmental toxicity. Refer to Section 4 "Pharmacology" above.
L420: ## 9.1 Fertility and Early Embryonic Development
L421: ## Study title: Intravenous Fertility Study of SCH 55700 in Mice.
L422: Study no.:	P-6981
Study report location:	EDR
Conducting laboratory and location:	Safety Evaluation Center
Schering-Plough Research Institute
Lafayette, NJ 07848
Date of study initiation:	February 25, 1998
GLP compliance:	Yes
QA statement:	Yes
Drug, lot #, and % purity:	SCH 55700; Lots #36809-090 (0.4 mg/mL), #36809-091 (2.0 mg/mL), #36809-092 (10 mg/mL); approximately 95% pure (0.4 mg/mL), 93% pure (2.0 mg/mL), and 90% pure (10 mg/mL)
L423: ## Key Study Findings
L424: - Male and female CD-1 mice were randomized into four groups: 0 (vehicle control), 2, 10, or 50 mg/kg reslizumab intravenously as a bolus injection into the tail vein. Males were given a single dose 28 and 14 days prior to mating and again on the first day of mating and on the 14th day after the start of mating. Females were given single doses 14 days prior to mating and again on the first day of mating and 14 days after.
- No drug-related effects were observed in any of the parameters evaluated: survival, clinical signs of toxicity, body weights and food consumption, gross lesions, male reproductive organ weights, and reproductive parameters.
- Although TK parameters were not calculated for this study, blood concentration in serum was measured and ADA analysis was conducted. A few animals (2 in the LD, 1 in the MD, and 2 in the HD; were positive for ADA and had no or very low reslizumab concentration in serum. In general, serum concentrations were higher on study day 22 compared to study day 30.
- The NOAEL for male and female fertility is the high-dose of 50 mg/kg.
L425: ## Methods
L426: Doses: 0, 2, 10, or 50 mg/kg reslizumab
Frequency of dosing: Males: dosed twice 28 and 14 days prior to mating and once at the beginning of the mating period and 14 days for those males that were still used for mating.
Females: dosed 14 days prior to mating, on the first day of mating, and again 14 days later for those females that did not mate during the first week. Those that mated during the second week were dosed on gestational day 0 (GD0).
Dose volume: 5 mL/kg
Route of administration: Intravenous (bolus injection into the tail vein).
L427: Formulation/Vehicle: However, no data were provided to confirm this. Sterile SCH 55700 placebo solution (formulation contained 70 mg/mL sucrose, 2.45 mg/mL sodium acetate trihydrate, and 0.12 mg/mL glacial acetic acid), Batch #35923-135-00.
Species/Strain: CD-1 mice
Number/Sex/Group: 25/sex/group
Satellite groups: None
Study design: Refer to Table 14 below, excerpted from the sponsor's submission.
Deviation from study protocol: Protocol deviations were not reported. The study report stated that there were no circumstances that may have affected the integrity of the data.

a: Males were dosed twice prior to mating (28 and 14 days) and once at the beginning of the mating period and 14 days for those males that were still used for mating. Females were dosed 14 days prior to mating, on the first day of the mating period and again 14 days later for those females that did not mate during the first week. Those that mated during the second week were dosed on gestation Day 0. The frequency of dosing (at approximately 14-day intervals) was based on the half-life of SCH 55700 in mice which is approximately two weeks.
L430: ## Rationale for Dose Selection
L431: The doses used in this study were based on a preliminary fertility and post-natal study in CD-1 mice (study #P-6370). In this study, CD-1 mice were randomized to receive 0, 2, 10, or 50 mg/kg reslizumab intravenously (tail vein) on the first day of the pre-mating period and again on the first day of the mating period. Females were re-dosed on gestation day 6 and on post-partum day 2. Results of this study showed a decrease in pregnancy rate in HD females compared to controls (75% in HD vs. 90% in controls). No other drug-related effects were observed.
L432: ## Observations and Results
L433: ## Mortality
L434: Animals were observed daily for survival. There were five deaths in the study, all in the LD group. Four males (animals #29, #31, #35, and #47) and one female (animal #149) died within 2 hours after receiving the third dose on study week 4. A cause of death was not determined for any of the animals. The study report states that “the deaths appeared to be due to an immunogenic response to a heterologous protein”. Therefore, these were not considered related to treatment.
L435: ## Clinical Signs
L436: Animals were observed daily for signs of toxicity to reslizumab. There were no drug-related effects observed.
L437: ## Body Weight
L438: Body weights were recorded once weekly for males and once weekly prior to mating and on days 0 (day of mating), 4, 7, and 14 after mating for females.
L439: There were no drug-related effects observed.
L440: ## Feed Consumption
L441: Food consumption was measured weekly during the pre-mating period for males and weekly prior to mating and during gestation (GD0-14) for females.
L442: There were no drug-related effects observed.
L443: ## Toxicokinetics
L444: Blood samples were collected from the abdominal aorta of all males for serum drug concentration and anti-drug antibody analysis days 16, 22, and 30 after the last dose. TK parameters (e.g., C_{max} and AUC) were not calculated in this study.
L445: The following animals were positive for anti-reslizumab antibodies: two in the LD (animals #43 and #48), one in the MD (animal #68), and two in the HD (animals #86 and #95). These animals had no or very low reslizumab concentration in serum, suggesting that the antibodies were neutralizing reslizumab in serum. The serum concentrations were higher on study day 22 compared to study day 30. Serum concentrations on study day 22 ranged from 3.8-14.6 µg/mL in the LD group, from 17-338.7 µg/mL in the MD group, and from 24.3-403.7 µg/mL in the HD group.
L446: ## Dosing Solution Analysis
L447: Results of dosing solution analyses were not provided in the final study report.
L448: ## Necropsy
L449: Males were sacrificed after completion of the mating period. Females were sacrificed on gestational day 14.
L450: There were no drug-related effects observed.
L451: ## Fertility Parameters
L452: ## Male Fertility
L453: Males were sacrificed approximately three or four weeks after the start of mating. Paired testes weights and epididymal weights were recorded for each animal.
L454: There were no drug-related changes in mating or fertility indices or reproductive organ weights in males.
L455: ## Female Fertility
L456: The vaginal cytology of all females was checked daily to determine if the estrous cycle was normal from 14 days prior to mating and during the mating period until evidence of mating was observed. Females were sacrificed on gestational day 14 (GD14) and the uteri and ovaries exposed to collect reproduction data (number of implantations, fetuses, resorptions, and corpora lutea). The uteri of apparently non-pregnant mice were also examined for evidence of implantations.
L457: There were no drug-related changes in any of the parameters evaluated in females.
L458: ## 9.2 Embryonic Fetal Development

L461: ## Key Study Findings
L462: - Pregnant CD-1 mice (25/group) received 0, 2, 10, or 50 mg/kg reslizumab intravenously once on gestational day 6 (GD6). Animals were dosed only once because reslizumab's half-life in general is approximately two weeks and this would cover the duration of the study.
- No drug-related effects were observed in any of the parameters evaluated in this study: survival, clinical signs of toxicity, maternal body weights, food consumption (qualitative), gross observations, reproduction parameters, and fetal malformations or developmental variations.
- The NOAEL for both maternal toxicity and teratogenicity is 50 mg/kg.
- Toxicokinetics were not conducted for this study.

L468: ## Rationale for Dose Selection
L469: Doses used in this EFD study (2, 10, and 50 mg/kg reslizumab) were selected based on a pilot intravenous embryo-fetal development study of SCH 55700 in CD-1 mice (study #D-27256). In this study, pregnant CD-1 mice (8/group) were treated with 0, 2, 10, or 50 mg/kg reslizumab intravenously (tail vein) once on gestational day 6. Pregnancy rate in the HD group was much lower compared to the vehicle control (37.5% vs. 87.5% in the controls). Therefore, the number of corpora lutea and implantation sites was also lower in the HD group relative to controls. However, the mean number of corpora lutea and implantation sites per pregnant mouse was similar across groups. There were no other drug-related changes observed in this study.
L470: ## Observations and Results
L471: ## Mortality
L472: Animals were observed daily for survival status. All animals survived for the duration of the study.
L473: ## Clinical Signs
L474: Animals were observed daily for any signs of toxicity. There were no drug-related changes observed.
L475: ## Body Weight
L476: Body weights were recorded on study days 0 (day of mating), and gestational days (GD) 6, 9, 12, 15, and 18 (day of necropsy). There were no drug-related changes observed.
L477: ## Feed Consumption
L478: Food consumption was measured on days 0 (mating), and GD 6, 10, 15, and 18. There were no drug-related changes observed.
L479: ## Toxicokinetics
L480: Toxicokinetics were not conducted in this study.
L481: ## Dosing Solution Analysis
L482: Results of dosing formulation analyses (concentration, homogeneity, stability) were not provided in the final study report.
L483: ## Necropsy
L484: Animals were sacrificed on GD18 by CO2 inhalation and the fetuses were removed. The dams were examined for external and visceral changes. The uteri of apparently non-pregnant mice were examined for evidence of implantations.
L485: There were no drug-related effects observed. The number of late resorptions in the MD group was higher than controls (6 in MD vs. 2 in controls), but a dose-response was not observed as the HD group had only 1 late resorption.
L486: ## Cesarean Section Data
L487: Animals were sacrificed on GD18. The numbers of corpora lutea, implantation sites, and resorptions were counted and distribution of the fetuses in the uterus was determined.

L491: ## Offspring Data
L492: Pups were sacrificed by CO2 inhalation after removal from the uterus. Approximately 50% of the fetuses from each litter were examined for soft tissue defects by serial slicing. The remaining pups were examined for gross visceral changes and processed for skeletal examination. Late resorptions and dead fetuses were also examined for external changes and, when possible, for skeletal changes. Fetal findings were
L493: classified as malformation3 or developmental variations4. All fetal remains were stored in glycerin and alcohol.
L494: There were no drug-related malformations observed in the study. There was a single incidence of testis not fully descended in the HD group (1 fetus in 1 litter) but this was considered probably unrelated to treatment due to the low incidence of the finding. Also, there was a higher incidence (litter and fetuses) of asymmetrical sternebra in treated animals compared to controls (but no dose-response). Because historical controls for CD-1 mice were not provided in the study report, an information request was sent to the sponsor on August 20, 2015 to request that they submit laboratory historical control data from age-matched animals to support their conclusion that this finding of asymmetrical sternebra is not related to reslizumab treatment.
L495: The sponsor's response was received on August 28, 2015, and it included historical control data for this finding, which is considered a common skeletal variation in mice. Although the incidence observed in this study is slightly higher than the historical control range, this was the only skeletal variation observed in drug-treated animals and the total incidence of skeletal variations in this study was lower in drug-treated animals compared to controls (60.3%, 39.7%, 41.3%, and 47.9% for control, LD, MD and HD; respectively). This provides sufficient justification for considering this finding as incidental.
L496: Refer to Table 17 below for a summary of skeletal observations.

L502: ## **Study title: Intravenous Embryo-Fetal Development Study of SCH 55700 in Rabbits.**
L503: Study no.:	P-6379
Study report location:	EDR
Conducting laboratory and location:	Safety Evaluation Center
Schering-Plough Research Institute
Lafayette, NJ 07848
Date of study initiation:	February 5, 1996
GLP compliance:	Yes
QA statement:	Yes
Drug, lot #:	SCH 55700; Lots #35923-113-01 (0.4 mg/mL), #35923-113-01 (2.0 mg/mL), and #35923-114-01 (10 mg/mL)
L504: ## **Key Study Findings**
L505: - Pregnant New Zealand rabbits (20/group) were treated with 0, 2, 10, or 50 mg/kg reslizumab intravenously on gestational day 7.
- There was a dose-dependent decrease in the number of corpora lutea and implantation sites in treated dams. This was also observed in the pilot study in rabbits.
- There were no drug-related effects on malformations or developmental variations in treated animals.
- The NOAEL for both maternal toxicity and teratogenicity is 50 mg/kg.
- Toxicokinetics were not conducted for this study.
L506: ## **Methods**
L507: Doses:	0, 2, 10, or 50 mg/kg reslizumab
Frequency of dosing:	Once on gestational day 7
Dose volume:	5 mL/kg
Route of administration:	Intravenous using an infusion pump into the marginal ear vein at a rate of approximately 0.24 mL/5 sec. (b) (4)
Formulation/Vehicle:	Sterile SCH 55700 placebo solution (formulation contained 70 mg/mL sucrose, 2.45 mg/mL sodium acetate trihydrate, and 0.12 mg/mL glacial acetic acid)
Species/Strain:	New Zealand white rabbits
Number/Sex/Group:	20 females/group
L508: Satellite groups: None.

L513: ## Rationale for Dose Selection
L514: The doses used in this study were selected based on data from a pilot intravenous embryo-fetal development study in rabbits (study #D-27243). Briefly, in study #D-27243, pregnant New Zealand white rabbits (4/group) received 0 (placebo reslizumab), 2, 10, or 50 mg/kg reslizumab intravenously (marginal ear vein) once on gestational day 7 using an infusion pump at a rate of 2.5-2.8 mL/minute. Results of this study show potential reslizumab-related effects in reproduction [i.e., decreased pregnancy rate (50% vs. 100% in the controls), resulting in a decreased number of corpora lutea and implantation sites in the HD group and increase in pre-implantation loss in the MD and HD groups] but no effects on fetal gross observations (malformations or developmental variations) compared to controls. The same doses (2, 10 and 50 mg/kg) were selected for the pivotal study.
L515: ## Observations and Results
L516: ## Mortality
L517: All dams were examined daily for survival. There were no drug-related effects in survival.
L518: ## Clinical Signs
L519: Animals were observed for any signs of toxicity. There were no drug-related effects on clinical signs.
L520: ## Body Weight
L521: Body weights were recorded on days 0 (day of mating), and GD7, GD10, GD13, GD16, GD19, GD22, GD25, GD28, and GD30.
L522: There were no drug-related effects observed in body weights in dams or offspring.
L523: ## **Feed Consumption**
L524: A visual estimate of food consumption was recorded daily for all rabbits.
L525: There were no drug-related effects observed in food consumption (qualitative).
L526: ## **Toxicokinetics**
L527: Toxicokinetics were not conducted for this study.
L528: ## **Dosing Solution Analysis**
L529: Results of dosing formulation analyses (concentration, homogeneity, stability) were not provided in the final study report.
L530: ## **Necropsy**
L531: On GD30, all rabbits were sacrificed, the fetuses removed and the uteri examined.
L532: There were no drug-related necropsy observations observed.
L533: ## **Cesarean Section Data**

In addition, there was a slight increase in the number of resorptions in animals at \ge 10 mg/kg reslizumab.
